Title: Vanguard Group boosts Strategy holdings by $50M to nearly $1B
URL: https://cryptobriefing.com/vanguard-strategy-holdings-billion/
Time Published: 2026-07-27T10:48:25Z
Full Content:
Searching... Via tradingkey.com The world's most famously crypto-skeptical asset manager now holds nearly $1 billion in the largest corporate Bitcoin treasury stock. Share Vanguard Group, the asset management giant that once blocked its clients from buying spot Bitcoin ETFs, just added another 529,105 shares of Strategy to its portfolio. That brings Vanguard’s total position to roughly 10.5 million shares worth $993.5 million. In English: the company that said “no thanks” to Bitcoin is now sitting on nearly $1 billion of the most Bitcoin-correlated stock on the market. The $50 million increase is, by Vanguard’s standards, a rounding error. The firm manages trillions of dollars across its index funds and ETFs. Strategy, formerly known as MicroStrategy before its rebrand, remains the single largest corporate holder of Bitcoin on the planet. The company has spent years converting its balance sheet into what is effectively a leveraged Bitcoin vehicle, accumulating hundreds of thousands of coins in the process. When spot Bitcoin ETFs launched in the US, Vanguard was conspicuously absent from the party, refusing to offer them on its brokerage platform. Vanguard is primarily a passive investor. Its funds track indexes. If Strategy is in the index, Vanguard buys it. The outcome is the same: nearly $1 billion of one of the world’s largest asset managers’ capital is now tied to Bitcoin’s price trajectory through a single stock. At various points, Vanguard’s MSTR holdings have reportedly exceeded 20 million shares, which would translate to more than 8% ownership of the company. The current 10.5 million share position suggests the firm has trimmed and rebuilt this stake multiple times as index weightings shift. Institutional ownership of MSTR surged in Q1 2026 despite the stock’s well-documented volatility. Strategy has made Bitcoin embedding its entire corporate strategy. Under Michael Saylor’s leadership, the company has issued billions in debt and equity to buy more Bitcoin, effectively turning MSTR into a publicly traded Bitcoin holding company. Every major index fund that includes MSTR becomes, by extension, a fractional Bitcoin holder. Strategy’s balance sheet is leveraged to Bitcoin in a way that amplifies both gains and losses. If Bitcoin enters another prolonged downturn, Vanguard’s $993.5 million position would shrink accordingly. Unlike an active manager who could cut the position on conviction, Vanguard’s passive funds would simply ride it down until index weightings adjusted. While Vanguard avoided the spot Bitcoin ETF race, rivals like BlackRock and Fidelity jumped in aggressively and captured billions in assets. Title: As New Era of Global Conflict Rages, Climate Change Turns the Prizes to Dust
URL: https://www.nakedcapitalism.com/2026/07/as-new-era-of-global-conflict-rages-climate-change-turns-the-prizes-to-dust.html
Time Published: 2026-07-27T10:00:20Z
Full Content:
The shifting pack ice, numbing temperatures, and powerful currents of the Antarctic’s Weddell Sea led Ernest Shackleton’s to describe it as the “worst portion of the worst sea in the world”. It made good on its reputation in 1915 when it sank Shackleton’s ship, Endurance, and those same conditions helped preserve it for more than a century. Discovered in 2022, Endurance is now becoming more accessible, however due to climate change. Those same climate forces making Shackleton’s ship easier to reach are also driving competition over shipping lanes and underwater minerals and fossil fuels. Indeed, many view global warming not as a threat, but as an opportunity. The Heritage Foundation, the crazy capitalist think tank responsible for Project 2025, argued in that screed that the NOAA’s work was “harmful to US prosperity.” And so away we go with Shackleton’s Endurance acting as a North Star. As the geopolitical wars rage over mineral and fossil fuel hot spots, and contests over shipping lanes and land corridors intensify, the world boils, ensuring that no one will emerge victorious aside from Mother Nature. A Vicious Spiral Let’s start near the opposite pole from Shackleton’s resting place where the competition over the Arctic heats up as the water literally does so. The journey from eastern China to England, which normally takes 40 days via the Suez Canal, more than 50 days around the Cape of Good Hope, and a complicated and expensive China-Europe rail route, can be cut in half by an accommodating Arctic route. As The Economist announces, “the Arctic will become more connected to the global economy.” That’s one way of putting it. Snow reflects 85 percent of the sunlight that hits it, exposed water only 7 percent. So as the Arctic melts, it speeds up the process for the remaining ice. The Arctic has been warming four times faster than anywhere else on the planet since 1979, according to multiple scientific studies. That means, according to a recent study in Communications Earth & Environment, that all ice-going vessels would only be unable to navigate this route in the spring come 2065, and that the route would be navigable year-round by 2100. Hooray! What often goes unmentioned in these pieces hyperventilating over how arctic shipping routes will transform the global economy is what exactly that means for the climate. And that’s not a pretty picture. Put simply, there won’t be much of a global economy left. Here are what some cities will look like as vessels breeze through the Arctic: If we want to really connect the Arctic to the economy, there’s always the ice-free future to consider. I imagine many/most of us look at those maps and recoil in horror. Project 2025 authors, capitalism’s most crazed supporters, and others cheerleading the expedited shipping through the Arctic see dollar signs. Needless to say, it’s not just sea level rise, but the breakdown of ocean circulation systems and weather patterns, bringing more severe weather, global food production collapse, and an inescapable doom loop. The Arctic’s increasing connection to the global economy is already helping to speed up the breakdown. From France24: It’s a vicious cycle. As sea ice melts and opens new routes for maritime traffic in the Arctic, the environmental fallout caused by vessels burning fossil fuels adds to global warming, which in turn melts more sea ice. A perfect example of this is black carbon. It’s a sooty material emitted from gas and diesel engines that aren’t completely combusted. Not only does black carbon pollute the air with particulate matter, but because of its ability to absorb light as heat, it contributes to climate change by warming the air. When black carbon is deposited on ice in the Arctic, it takes away its ability to reflect heat. “The black colour deposited on the white ice means the ice then absorbs more sunlight, leading to more melting,” explained Sammie Buzzard, a polar scientist at the Centre for Polar Observation and Modelling at Northumbria University. *** Elsewhere, similar geoeconomic battles are playing out over transit corridors that are likely to be rendered useless—or at least not worth the investment— by climate change. The Caspian Sea is currently a focal point of the Middle Corridor, to which the Trump Route for International peace and Prosperity aspires to link up to. The geopolitical contest over Central Asian minerals, control over the Caucasus, and energy flows from the Caspian region are seeing tensions rise and increase the risk of conflict. Meanwhile the Caspian Sea is dying. From SciTech Daily: Water levels in the Caspian Sea, the largest landlocked body of water in the world, are steadily falling as rising temperatures cause more evaporation than inflow. Even if global warming is kept below 2°C, scientists estimate the sea could drop by 5 to 10 meters. If temperatures rise further, the decline could reach up to 21 meters by the end of the century. In the northern Caspian, the findings show that some settlements, ports, and industrial facilities could end up being stranded tens or even hundreds of kilometers from new shorelines. The exposed dry seabed is likely to release dust containing industrial contaminants and salt, posing serious threats to human health, as previously occurred with the drying of the Aral Sea. The findings also predict a concerning trajectory for industry, with critical ports in all bordering countries being impacted. Baku (Azerbaijan), Anzali (Iran), and Aktau (Kazakhstan) are expected to experience increases in distance to shore of 1 km or more, while Turkmenbashi (Turkmenistan), and Lagan (Russia; planned future site), could see increases of 16 km and 115 km respectively… Some cities around the Caspian use desalination (removing salt and minerals from saltwater) for freshwater supplies, and water level declines are already impacting desalination operations at Aktau in Kazakhstan. Loss of water from the Caspian could also affect the regional climate, leading to reduced rainfall and drier conditions across central Asia with indirect impacts on agriculture. Further east where the “Great Game” is once again playing out, water is increasingly hard to come by, and it won’t be long before Mackinder’s Heartland Theory needs a Mad Max-style update. From IOP Science: The year 2025, designated by the United Nations as the ‘International Year of Glaciers’ Preservation’, ironically became the most negative glacier mass-balance year on record in Central Asia. In situobservations reveal exceptionally strong and spatially coherent ice losses across the region, although not all individual glaciers experienced record losses. Regional simulations with the Global Glacier Evolution Model support these findings, indicating that 64% of glaciers larger than 1 km2(∼4000 glaciers) reached their most negative annual mass balance within the study period. Within a single year, glaciers in Central Asia lost an estimated 30 ± 6 km3 of ice (26 ± 9 Gt), corresponding to ∼2% of their present-day total mass. Mass loss was strongest in the western Tien Shan and western Pamir (−2% to −4%) and more moderate in the eastern Tien Shan and eastern Pamir (−1% to −1.5%). Climate reanalysis attributes the extreme 2025 losses to persistently above-average spring and summer temperatures, an unusually early onset of the melt season, and reduced snowfall frequency during late spring and summer. Together, these conditions enhanced melt-amplifying feedbacks by accelerating snow loss, leading to earlier exposure of low-albedo ice surfaces. The 2025 losses mark an alarming milestone for Central Asian glaciers and may become the new normal in the future. To add a little more perspective, approximately 81 percent of Central Asia’s population depends on water originating in the Tien Shan and Pamir. And as supplies dwindle it increases the chances of internal destabilization and external conflict, which equals instability across food systems, energy markets, and regional trade. The Panama Canal This choke point is at the heart of the US and China struggle for control over global shipping infrastructure. Under pressure from Washington, Panama “retook control” of the Pacific Ocean port of Balboa and the Atlantic Ocean port of Cristobal after a court annulled a contract that had allowed a subsidiary of Hong Kong-based CK Hutchison Holdings to operate both since 1997. Beijing has retaliated by detaining Panama-flagged vessels, and Chinese shipping giant Cosco, which owns one of the world’s largest fleets of tankers, suspended operations at the two busiest Panama Canal ports. This fight is playing out following a Blackrock consortium’s attempt—with an assist from Washington—to buy up dozens of ports from CK Hutchison. Beijing has nixed the deal over concerns that the new ownership could, under direction from Washington, raise prices on or refuse Chinese ships or allow the US to militarize the ports and use them to enact a maritime blockade of China. Whoever ultimately controls the Panama ports might not have much to celebrate. The Panama Canal Authority is already reducing maximum navigable drafts in anticipation of the coming El Nino weather pattern. Under “normal” water level conditions, the Canal can handle 34 to 38 daily transits, but that had be cut by about 30 percent in 2023-24 due to the last El Nino when October 2023 saw the least amount of rainfall in recorded history. This upcoming El Nino is expected to be much worse. *** From where I sit, the fault for this race toward our own destruction lies almost exclusively in Washington and the transnational oligarchs it serves. Other countries have shown themselves willing to cooperate and compromise, but if China, for example, emerges as the new hegemon, would it chart an effective degrowth course on climate change or would it too seek growth at all costs? Many apply the Thucydides Trap to US-China positions to help explain US elite determination for us all to suffer these pyrrhic contests, but perhaps that term is being applied to the wrong fight. When Herman Wouk, the novelist and World War II veteran, popularized the Thucydides Trap in 1980 to describe the Cold War, he said this: How do we break out of this Thucydidean trap, which now threatens to strangle, if not to destroy, our world? To my ear, that sounds just as much about the climate catastrophe unfolding as the threat of nuclear war. Cursed are we to confront both under a system that deems both acceptable risks (if not welcomed outcomes) in the quest to squeeze every last cent out of the planet and its inhabitants. Thank you for this work Conor Gallagher! It’s nice to have a trusted source for such important information gathering. Maybe I missed it, but could you provide the link for the source of the maps from national geographic? I’d like to see the southern hemisphere, if they included it. Please include the dates on the National Geographic projections. Thanks For some reason can’t track down the original now. Here’s a secondary source with more of southern hemisphere though: https://geoengineeringwatch.org/what-the-world-would-look-like-if-all-the-ice-melted/ If the Heritage Society didn’t make everything about profits and money then they’d have to go out and get real jobs–clearly unacceptable. They are useless eaters but the “die quickly” doctrine only applies to the Untermenchen. A friend who lives in Arizona is about to install her third air conditioner replace in as many decades. She says the desert state is becoming almost unlivable during the ever expanding hot months. So it’s not only the elites who defy ecology but also those ordinaries who depend on refrigeration instead of the thick adobe walls that once provided some protection from the unfavorable climate. Wesley Powell predicted it all back in the 19th c. Is nemesis succeeding hubris? There’s nothing but blue skies and good times ahead! Oh,wait. The odds of the climate crisis being addressed in a rational manner are somewhat worse than the odds of winning the lottery. It’s important to keep in mind that these changes are not linear and there are still unknown feedback loops that will become evident in time. IBG/YBG is the cynical mantra of the neoliberal accelerationists and nihilists who have turned their unmitigated greed and lust for personal gratification into a murderous cult manifested by their paid shills at the Heritage Foundation. Celebrating the rapid melting of the polar ice caps is conclusive proof that Thiel and his ilk are the moral equivalents of Ted Bundy or Jeffrey Dahmer. I find credible those who assert that the climate crisis is just one aspect of the ecological overshoot situation. When the Club of Rome’s Limits to Growth work came out, I was in my twenties and didn’t give it much attention. Now, decades later, I have been looking at stuff by “recovering astrophysicist” Tom Murphy, ecologist Bill Rees, and historians of science Naomi Oreskes and Erik Conway. It seems to me that people are clever enough to have produced the anthropogenic global warming carbon pulse, and the vast multiplication in the physical work that can be done when calling on the power stored in ancient sunlight. People are also clever enough for a few of them to have figured out, decades ago, the necessity to change our techno-industrial ways to avoid negative outcomes. However, it seems clear that people are NOT capable of reducing the burning of fossil carbon rapidly enough to avoid the catastrophic consequences already baked in, although having international meetings about it and making promises concerning the topic are within our collective power. From one point of view, this is tragic. From another point of view, it’s physics. A few years ago I came across the surrealist poem by James Tate titled “I Left My Couch in Tatamagouche”. It is set in Nova Scotia, and I am half Nova Scotian by descent. The story is of a person who takes their green couch with them everywhere they go – we begin in media res: “I desired lemonade— It was hot and I had been walking for hours— but after much wrestling, pushing and shoving, I simply could not get my couch through the restaurant door.” Even with help from the owner, the owner’s son, and other customers, it can’t be done. Finally it’s closing time, and the narrator urges the people to go home. That night, while sleeping peacefully outside the train station on my little green couch, I met a giantess by the name of Anna Swan. She knelt beside my couch and stroked my brow with tenderness. She was like a mother to me for a few moments there under the night sky. Anna Swan was a real historical personage of the nineteenth century from that area: she was a very tall girl, a world-traveling entertainer employed by P.T. Barnum, the bride of a similarly statured Confederate veteran, and finally a farm wife in Ohio. In the poem, no conversation is reported. Neverthless, it seems the encounter has had an effect on the narrator: In the morning, I left my couch in Tatamagouche, and that has made a big difference. I decided to modify this poem, to ask Anna to give a dharma talk on how to live with awareness and with love, even while thinking that “modernity is metastatic” and that the process of the degradation of environmental conditions is already underway. It seemed to work, as a poem – Anna, in “a preposterous conversation”, could speak about the application of the Four Noble Truths to the unsatisfactoriness of this situation, the recognition that it is natural, but exhausting, to wish that things would stay as they were, and that it is normal for things which had beginnings to have endings as well. And also, of course, in the meantime one is still here, still able to enjoy what is enjoyable, and to do those things which are do-able. And, as Yogi Berra didn’t say – and Anna Swan didn’t either – “you never know when something surprising might happen.” Your email address will not be published. Title: The Technocratic Control of USrael
URL: https://www.activistpost.com/the-technocratic-control-of-usrael/
Time Published: 2026-07-27T10:00:00Z
Full Content:
Also available on Substack. * * *In the United States (US) a gaggle of Silicon Valley oligarchs has formed a partnership that has seized operational control of the US defence sector. This group of oligarchs can be distinguished by their liking for the ideas presented in Nick Land’s Dark Enlightenment and their enthusiasm for Technocracy. They want to build what their guru Curtis Yarvin called a “patchwork of realms” in the form of a transnational network of privately owned city-states, or neostates. Inspired by both the Dark Enlightenment and Technocracy, a wider Neoreactionary movement (NRx) has formed, and the Silicon Valley oligarchs are its inspirational leaders. The NRx oligarchs’ investment strategy focuses on seed funding “disruptive technology” startup ventures. In keeping with the Dark Enlightenment, this is called accelerationism. The NRx-aligned oligarchs—often referred to as the PayPal Mafia—include Peter Thiel (Palantir Technologies), Marc Andreessen (Andreessen Horowitz), Elon Musk (X), Sam Altman (Open AI), and others. In 2023 Marc Andreessen published his Techno-Optimists Manifesto, in which he said, “[W]e believe in accelerationism – the conscious and deliberate propulsion of technological development.” Therefore, we’ll refer to this group of Silicon Valley oligarchs as the NRx accelerationists. US Secretary of War Pete Hegseth recently announced the appointments made to the newly reconstituted Defense Policy Board (DPD). NRx accelerationist presence on the DPD is notable. The NRx accelerationists’ commitment to constructing a global patchwork of city-state realms has found favour with a wider cohort of “American elites”—US-based oligarchs. Their interests are represented by the Heritage Foundation (HF), and, combined with the NRx accelerationists, they have comprehensive control of the DPD. The DPD’s remit is to provide “independent strategic advice and recommendations” on such matters as US military “strategic planning, the policy implications of [US] force structure and modernization.” Conspicuously, the board will provide independent guidance on “regional defense policies” just as a new multipolar regional balance of power global governance system emerges. At the same time, the Trump administration—which the NRx accelerationists control—is pushing through legislative budget amendments to form what journalist and author James Corbett justly calls USrael. Section 224 (now Sec. 219) of the annual US National Defense Authorization Act (NDAA) budget renewal establishes the “United States-Israel Defense Technology Cooperation Initiative”. Noting the effect of Sec. 224/219, the director of the Democratizing Foreign Policy programme at the Quincy Institute, Ben Freeman, wrote: Section 224 lays the groundwork for bilateral research and development, co-production of weapons, joint ventures, licensing agreements, and seemingly every manner of U.S.-Israeli military-industrial complex cooperation.[. . .] [T]his provision would greatly expand coordination to seemingly every area of defense tech, including AI, quantum, autonomous systems, directed energy, cyber, biotech, and many more. It also proposes “network integration” and “data fusion.” In other words, the U.S. military’s data could soon be the Israeli military’s data. [. . .] It would fuse the U.S. and Israeli defense sectors in multiple areas vital to the battlefields of the future, like autonomous systems and cyber. In other words, just as USrael is rising, the NRx accelerationists and their “conservative” HF oligarch partners are positioning themselves to reap the benefits of the fusion of the US and Israeli defence sectors.The US Senate is divided along party lines and has temporarilly blocked the 2027 NDAA renewal, which is usually a formality. Though some have expressed concern about Sec. 219, the obstruction of the Bill is due more to Democrat disquiet over the size of Trump’s $1.15 Trillion defense budget request and the fact that Trump has ignored the necessary house debate for continuence of the conflict with Iran. Bipartisan support for diverting US taxpayers money to Israel remains strong and the recent State Department budget renewal providing a further $3.3 billion to Israel easily passed the vote. But there are a growinng number of Democrat voices expressing concerns about the US’ blanket support of the Israeli government, seemigly, no matter what it does. To be frank, none of that really matters to people like Billionnaire Tech-investor Marc Andreessen. He’s is not an elected politican but has been appointed to the DPD nonetheless. He also sits on the President’s Council of Advisors on Science and Technology (PCAST). Andreessen openly stated that the main reason he supported Trump was because his policies were more favourable to NRx accelerationist interests. He assisted the Trump administration to fill US government positions, and now Andreessen has been appointed to advise the Federal Reserve on what monetary policy it should implement. But Andreessen is far from the only DPD member with a clear conflict of interest. Michael Anton was a former managing director at BlackRock and a senior executive at Citigroup. Christopher A. Williams, the former chair of the old DPD, runs a private consultancy firm that provides strategic national security and technology advice to American businesses. Hegseth’s DPD is heavily influenced by the Heritage Foundation. Michael P. Pillsbury is a senior fellow for China strategy at the HF. A known “China Hawk”, Pillsbury was mentored by Zbigniew Brzezinski, who wrote the book Between Two Ages: America’s Role in the Technotronic Era. The book provided an American exceptionalist rationale for the adoption of Technocracy, both in the US and internationally. Brzezinski then formed the Trilateral Commission with oligarch David Rockefeller to bring about the three-power world planned by the Anglo-American establishment between the wars—a multipolar regional balance of power system. John Klyczek, writing for Unlimited Hangout, observed that the HF has formed a partnership with the PayPal Mafia. The shared purpose of the HF and the PayPal Mafia is to subject American schoolchildren to the global digital transformation and accustom them to using what the United Nations (UN) calls “frontier technology”. At the same time, through a manipulative educational grant system, using centralised financial technology (fintech), they seek to control national curriculums and socially engineer American children. I previously reported that the HF is backed by a network of US-based oligarchs, such as the Coors, Koch, Uihlein, Barre Seid, Bradley, and Scaiffe families. They are behind the Project 2025 Promise to America, assuring US citizens that the Trump administration—that the HF oligarchs helped to manoeuvre into office—would “put the interests of the everyday American over the desires of the ruling elite.” This was a cosmic irony. Project 2025 set the agenda for Trump’s first 180 days in office. During that time, Trump issued a slew of Executive Orders, the vast majority of which were practically written beforehand by the HF-aligned “ruling elite”. New DPD appointee, Theo J. Wold, is a fellow of the HF who, in another almost unbelievable irony, supposedly advises the HF on their design of US government policy to protect Americans “against Big Tech abuses” while simultaneously acting as legal council for Palantir Technologies. Wold is joined on the DPD by Michael N. Anton and Rachel A. Bovard, who were also embedded within the HF-led Project 2025. Given that the DPD mandate includes designing—let’s be honest—the US government’s “regional defense policies”, its anti-China hawkishness and HF/Project 2025 stacking should be concerning for Palestinians but also staunch Zionists. In terms of foreign policy objectives for the Middle East “region”, Project 2025’s Conservative Promise was not simply based upon defending Israel’s interests. Though Project 2025 wanted the US to continue to provide financial and military support to the Israeli government, it had other aims for the Middle East “region”. The HF/Project 2025 also sought to use US foreign policy strategically to empower its “Gulf partners to take responsibility for their own coastal, air, and missile defenses both individually and working collectively.”The HF emphasis was “to build a Middle East security pact that includes Israel, Egypt, the Gulf states, and potentially India”; to encourage “bilateral development relationships” between, for example, Israel and the UAE, Kuwait, and Qatar; and to promote “investment between Israel and its Arab neighbors,” while economically isolating “states allied to Iran”. HF/Project 2025 is ardently anti-China. Describing the Chinese government as “a totalitarian enemy of the United States” and admonishing the “Big Tech” firms that have allowed themselves to become “a tool of China’s government”. HF/Project 2025 decries the “America’s elites” who, by collaborating with China, have, it claims, “betrayed the American people”. The HF oligarchs say they are broadly opposed to the United Nations and other intergovernmental organisations and alliances, even NATO, which, they say, should be used “as a means to attain defined [American] goals”. The HF/Project 2025 Conservative Promise has significant overlaps with Marc Andreessen’s Techo-Optimists Manifesto, which he published six months later in October 2023. This isn’t surprising because a key goal of HF philanthropy is to “spark creative disruption”, which oligarchs like Andreessen call accelerationism. Whether Andreessen personally used the Project 2025 database—of HF-linked conservative officials—for filling Trump administration posts is unknown. But it was apparently used nonetheless. HF/Project 2025, despite its conservative pretensions, is fully behind the radical transformation of society favoured by accelerationists like Andreessen. The so-called architect of Project 2025, Paul Dans, who served as the director of the HF’s Presidential Transition Project, was eager to push the concept of “Freedom Cities”—American Special Economic Zones intended for smart city development. Dans said the Freedom City “was an intriguing Trump idea”, though it wasn’t Trump’s idea at all. It came straight out of the Heritage Foundation. As I previously reported for Unlimited Hangout: Trump’s long-time economic advisor Stephen Moore supports the city-state model. Moore is the former chief economist at the Heritage Foundation [which] was behind the State Policy Network (SPN) that proposed the “Compact For America” shortly before Trump’s first US election win in 2016. What Trump termed Freedom Cities in 2023, the Compact for America, whose National Spokesman was Stephen Moore, called Prosperity Districts in 2015. Consequently, Marc Andreessen will join a majority of Defense Policy Board members who want to accelerate American innovation using his Dark Enlightenment-based NRx model. But this is an international project, not one limited to the US. With oversight of USrael defence policy, the potential exists to use military power to make that global project succeed. Fusing the U.S. military’s data with the Israeli military’s data facilitates the NRx accelerationists’ development of their digital kill chains. It is the NRx accelerationists’ tech firms who are, as Freeman observed, advancing “AI, quantum, autonomous systems, directed energy, cyber, [and] biotech” weapons technology. Regardless of US-Israeli international relations, the NRx accelerationists have their own strategic partnership with the Israeli defence sector and their own private sector working arrangements with Israeli Unit 8200 and its globally dispersed alumni. Sec. 219 primarilly serves their interests. Rounding off the accelerationists and anti-China hawks on the DPD is Blake Masters, a protégé of Peter Thiel and a co-author, with Thiel, of Zero to One: Notes on Startups, or How to Build the Future. Very firmly Thiel’s man, Masters was chief operating officer at Thiel Capital and president of the Thiel Foundation. Thiel then bankrolled Masters’ miserable political career, which, despite the endorsements and support from Thiel, Trump, and another Thiel protégé, J.D. Vance, Masters consistently managed to foul up. Masters has no qualifications or experience that would seemingly warrant his appointment to the DPD other than being Thiel’s representative. If the proposed section Sec. 219 eventually passes, which seems highly likely, it will certainly create USrael. But the chief beneficiaries of these developments are the NRx accelerationists and their transnational oligarch partners.

Title: George Gibney due back before court for sentencing hearing
URL: https://www.irishtimes.com/crime-law/courts/2026/07/27/george-gibney-due-back-before-court-for-sentencing-hearing/
Time Published: 2026-07-27T08:51:49Z
Full Content:
A woman sexually abused by former swim coach George Gibney has said she gets to walk from court “free”, while the world will know him as a “convicted, serial paedophile”. She told the Central Criminal Court that his abuse of her some 50 years ago caused “more than just a loss of innocence”; it brought about the “destruction of a previously happy and healthy childhood”. “You were a person in authority. People respected and trusted you and you abused that position ... You took and took as you saw fit without a thought for the immediate or lifelong impact,” she told Gibney, who looked ahead into space from his wheelchair. The woman was one of four found by a jury to have been abused by the coach as girls between the ages of eight and 13 about 50 years ago. A jury earlier this month found the 78-year-old guilty of 39 counts of indecent assault and one count of attempted rape on dates between 1971 and 1981 at various locations, including at two swimming pools in south Co Dublin. The attempted rape took place in a changing room at Newpark Sports Centre in Blackrock, Dublin. [ The George Gibney trial: ‘Predatory’ abuser operated at heart of Irish swimmingOpens in new window ] Gibney’s senior counsel, Patrick Gageby, told the court on Monday that the former coach accepts the jury’s verdict and will not appeal against his convictions. “You have a very different person in front of you than the person who committed these offences against very young children in his care,” counsel said as part of submissions to Judge Mícheál O’Higgins during a sentencing hearing. The Director of Public Prosecutions contended that the offences fall within the upper range of offending due in part to Gibney’s authoritative position and the age of the victims. The judge said he would take time to consider the victim-impact statements delivered by the four victims. He remanded Gibney in custody pending sentencing in October. The trial heard Gibney gave some of the girls lifts to swimming and abused them in his car. Further abuse of two sisters happened in his home when they were babysitting his children. The assaults included French kissing, inappropriate touching, digital penetration and forced oral sex. In a victim-impact statement, one of the survivors said she needed psychological intervention from the age of nine, had nightmares, isolated herself and self-harmed due to his actions. Sports were a big part of her family, and swimming was her “absolute joy”. She said he robbed her potential and she never found out if she would have ended up a good, great or mediocre athlete. He changed the trajectory of her life, and “absolutely not in the ways I wanted”, she said. She had to watch him being “lauded” and “hero worshipped” by the establishment while knowing the truth about his “evilness”. “The world is going to know you now as a convicted serial paedophile, while I get to walk out of here free, free of you,” she said. Another survivor said she finally felt “safe and championed” after being believed by gardaí when she reported the abuse in recent years. She outlined to the court how her life has been negatively shaped by Gibney’s actions. She became “almost unreachable” and an “observer” who never fully participated in life. A third woman said her 13-year-old self has been “frozen in time” and she is not sure if she will ever be released. Her overwhelming emotion was anger at what Gibney did to her decades ago and how this has affected her relationships. She said the trial was “traumatising”, and she criticised press coverage of it. The law needs to be changed so it respects the dignity and courage of victims, she said. The fourth woman read a victim-impact statement but asked for her privacy to be respected. Gibney had been charged with 79 counts. At the outset of the trial he pleaded guilty to five counts of indecent assault, while the judge threw out 33 charges after the prosecution’s evidence was heard. The jury acquitted Gibney of one indecent assault count. Title: DCC board backs ‘compelling’ takeover bid of up to £5.73 billion
URL: https://www.irishtimes.com/business/2026/07/27/dcc-board-backs-compelling-takeover-bid-of-up-to-573-billion/
Time Published: 2026-07-27T06:46:45Z
Full Content:
DCC Energy chief executive Donal Murphy said he expects the vast majority of shareholders to support the sale of the group to two US private equity firms in a deal worth up to £5.73 billion (€6.71 billion). Murphy, though, finds himself on a collision course with some top investors resisting the deal. The Dublin-based group confirmed on Monday it had agreed to sell itself to KKR and Energy Capital. The transaction that includes a £65.25-a-share upfront payment and £1.25 extra per share if they achieve at least $800 million (€704 million) from a sale of the tech division, known as Nexora. The bid consortium had included the payment of DCC’s final €1.47-a-share dividend on its earnings for last year in each of the multiple offers it tabled since late April. The dividend was included in the statement on the agreed deal on Monday, even though it had actually been paid out last week. Murphy said this was to allow the market compare each offer from the time DCC rejected an initial €58-a-share bid. The proposal values the upfront cash offer at a 21.3 per cent premium to DCC’s closing share price in London – where it is listed – on April 28th, before it emerged that KKR and Energy Capital were circling the group. “We believe the majority of shareholders will vote in favour of this,” Murphy told The Irish Times, adding that the board had “engaged very robustly” with the suitors over the past three months. DCC is one of three Irish public companies subject to takeover proposals. PTSB and Irish Continental Group are also under offer. Murphy has overseen a strategy to abandon DCC’s conglomerate roots to focus on energy. He said that while the board “absolutely believes” the remaining energy division is on course to double its operating profit to £830 million between 2022 and 2030, the target is “not without risk” when weighted against an uncertain geopolitical and macroeconomic backdrop as well as the need for the group to invest £1 billion-£1.2 billion. “When the board looks at it and evaluates it in totality, we believe that this is very compelling from a shareholder perspective and that’s why we recommended it,” said Murphy. Five big shareholders – Fidelity International (FIL), DCC’s founder Jim Flavin, Aviva Investors, Marathon Asset Management and Ninety One – have signalled in recent times that they would not support an offer around the level tabled by the consortium. They own more than 15 per cent of the stock between them. Alex Wright, portfolio manager at FIL which owns 6.9 per cent of DCC, said on Monday that his position remains unchanged from earlier this month, when he said he would only entertain a bid of a least £70 a share. Flavin also said he would continue to campaign against a sale. DCC, whose businesses once spanned Robert Roberts tea and coffee to waste management, decided in late 2024 to abandon the remaining elements of its conglomerate roots. DCC sold its healthcare unit a year ago. It also offloaded part of its technology business, with the remainder of that division, Nexora, currently on the market and expected to be sold by the end of this year. Murphy said that despite extensive engagement with the market and the strategic progress DCC has made in recent years, the FTSE 100 company had not enjoyed an expected valuation bump in the public market. DCC – whose energy businesses span the operation of 1,173 petrol stations in Europe to distributing liquid gas on both sides of the Atlantic, and installing and maintaining solar PV and heat pump systems – had tweaked the narrative in efforts to secure a re-rating. While it once put greater emphasis on how it fit into the environment, social and governance (ESG) agenda as a play on the energy transition, it had put more weight recently on security and affordability of fuel supply, according to analysts. “The evidence has been that this transition is way, way, way slower than anyone anticipated. When we set out in 2022 with our strategy, we believed and the world believed that energy transition was going to happen very quickly. Energy transition is happening very slowly.” Murphy said DCC has faced difficulties attracting new investors in recent times as they have preferred to wait for evidence of how the energy strategy will evolve. DCC shares were up 1.2 per cent at £63.60 in late morning trading on Monday. Share Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) saw a notable $33 million in net inflows last week, reflecting a renewed interest in Bitcoin through traditional financial markets. This inflow comes amid a broader context of fluctuating demand, as previous weeks in July recorded significantly higher inflows of $75.7 million and $197.4 million. The inflows suggest a reversal of the earlier trend in 2026, where Bitcoin ETFs experienced a series of outflows. This shift in investor behavior could indicate a growing confidence in Bitcoin as a viable asset within these traditional markets. The inflows may also have implications for Bitcoin’s price trajectory, potentially affecting market sentiment and the likelihood of Bitcoin exceeding key price thresholds in the short term. Current market indicators show a high level of confidence that Bitcoin will remain above $60,000 by July 28, with some markets pricing in a 99.6% chance of this outcome. Time Published: 2026-07-27T01:00:00Z
Full Content:
PR TIMESのご利用について 資料をダウンロード 株式会社DeFimans 株式会社DeFimans（所在地：東京都港区、代表取締役：小野 暢思・佐藤 太思）とThe Capital Summitは、2026年7月15日（水）、CIC Tokyo（虎ノ門ヒルズ ビジネスタワー15階）にて、機関投資家・金融機関・デジタルアセット業界の意思決定者を対象としたカンファレンス「The Capital Summit Tokyo」を共同開催しました。 当日は、金融機関、資産運用会社、暗号資産交換業者、ブロックチェーンインフラ企業、web3プロジェクトなど、多様なバックグラウンドを持つ参加者が来場し、会場は終日活発な議論と交流で賑わいました。 各セッションでは、グローバル・ステーブルコイン戦略、スポットETF、クロスボーダー決済、機関投資家向けカストディ、暗号資産交換業の将来、RWA（Real Jonathan Chang 氏 トラストレス・ビットコイン・ヴォールト — カストディを手放さないネイティブBTC担保 Babylon Labs CTO Vitalis Salis 氏 なお、本イベントで実施した各セッションの詳細については、セッションレポートとして順次公開予定です。 各登壇者による議論の内容や注目ポイントを詳しくご紹介します。 今回のイベントでは、以下のような市場トレンドが共通テーマとして挙げられました。 機関投資家によるデジタルアセット市場への参入拡大 ステーブルコインを活用した決済・送金インフラの高度化 トークン化資産（RWA）の実用フェーズへの移行 ビットコインを担保とした新たな金融サービスの発展 日本市場への期待と、グローバル市場との連携強化 厳選された国内外の意思決定者や実務責任者が一堂に会し、日本市場の将来像について率直な議論と活発な交流が行われました。 今年の議論を通じて共通していたテーマは、日本のデジタルアセット市場が「制度設計・PoC（実証実験）」の段階から、「社会実装・事業化」へと着実に移行し始めているということです。数年前までは「規制が整うか」が中心的な論点でしたが、現在では「どのような金融商品・サービスを、どのインフラ上で提供するか」が議論の中心となり、実装を前提とした具体的な議論が数多く交わされました。 特に、ステーブルコイン、トークン化資産（RWA）、機関投資家向けカストディ、Bitcoinを活用した新たな金融インフラ、そしてETF・トークン化ファンドなど、従来は個別に語られてきたテーマが、一つの金融エコシステムとして議論されたことは象徴的でした。デジタルアセットは単独のプロダクトではなく、決済・資産運用・市場インフラ・担保・流動性を横断する金融基盤として捉えられ始めており、日本市場もその転換点を迎えています。 また、多くの海外プロジェクトからは、日本市場への期待の高さもうかがえました。制度の明確性や金融機関との協業環境を評価する声が多く聞かれ、イベント当日は金融機関、Web3プロジェクト、インフラ企業、投資家による数多くの1on1ミーティングやネットワーキングが実施されました。単なる情報交換に留まらず、新たなPoCや事業提携、国内展開に向けた具体的な協議へと発展する対話が各所で生まれ、日本がグローバルデジタルアセット市場における重要な拠点として存在感を高めつつあることを印象付けました。 The Capital Summit Tokyoは、最新トレンドを共有する場であると同時に、日本と世界をつなぎ、新たな事業やパートナーシップが生まれるためのプラットフォームとして開催されました。DeFimansは今後も国内外のプレイヤーを結び付ける場を創出し、日本のデジタルアセット市場の発展とグローバルエコシステムとの接続に貢献してまいります。 ▪️タイトルスポンサー Tether（テザー）は、世界最大のステーブルコインであるUSDTの発行会社です。流通額は1,800億米ドルを超え、日々の決済ボリュームは大手カードネットワークに匹敵する規模を誇ります。デジタル金融における支配的な決済資産であり、新興国におけるドル資金フローの主要な決済インフラとしての役割を担っています。 ウェブサイト: tether.to ▪️ゴールドスポンサー RWA（Real World Assets）やオンチェーン金融向けに開発された、機関投資家向けLayer 1ブロックチェーンです。コンプライアンス対応、高い処理性能、既存の金融インフラとの相互運用性を重視して設計されています。 ウェブサイト: pharos.xyz Babylon Labsは、世界初のトラストレスかつセルフカストディ型のビットコインステーキングプロトコルを開発しました。これにより、ビットコイン保有者は、PoSチェーン、L2、データアベイラビリティ（DA）レイヤーなどのプルーフ・オブ・ステーク（PoS）システム上でBTCをステーキングでき、第三者によるカストディ、ブリッジソリューション、ラッピングサービスなしで、ステーキング報酬を得ることが可能になります。 ウェブサイト：https://babylonlabs.io/ ▪️シルバースポンサー BitGoは、機関投資家向けデジタル資産カストディおよびセキュリティインフラの分野をリードする企業です。複数の法域にわたり、規制当局の認可を受けたカストディおよびウォレットソリューションを提供しており、機関投資家向けコールドストレージとMPC(マルチパーティ計算)ハイブリッドモデルの分野において最も早期に取り組んだプレイヤーです。 ウェブサイト: www.bitgo.com Nethermindは、イーサリアムのコアインフラおよびリサーチを手がける企業であり、ブロックチェーンを稼働させる基盤ソフトウェア(Execution Client)、セキュリティ、ゼロ知識証明(ZK)関連ツールの開発を行っています。オンチェーン領域で事業を展開する機関投資家にとって、重要なテクノロジーパートナーです。 ウェブサイト: www.nethermind.io The Capital Summit The Capital Summitは、投資・市場構造・資本形成に特化した完全招待制のグローバル機関投資家向けフォーラムです。東京、香港、ソウル、シンガポール、ロンドンを中心に開催されるクロスボーダーカンファレンスシリーズとして、世界のアロケーター、資産運用会社、金融機関、インフラ事業者を結び付けています。 公式サイト：https://thecapitalsummit.ai/ X：https://x.com/CapSummitGlobal 株式会社DeFimans Title: Oil asset protection projected to boost licensing round success
URL: https://punchng.com/oil-asset-protection-projected-to-boost-licensing-round-success/
Time Published: 2026-07-27T00:54:24Z
Full Content:
Most Widely Read Newspaper Kindly share this story: Nigeria’s ambitious 2025 oil licensing round is expected to benefit from sustained oil asset protection, as stakeholders emphasise pipeline security and Niger Delta stability to attract investment, DARE OLAWIN writes The country’s 2025 upstream oil licensing round has set ambitious production and reserve targets, with the Nigerian Upstream Petroleum Regulatory Commission seeking to add 500 million barrels to the country’s reserves and increase crude oil production by 300,000 barrels per day within three years. Industry stakeholders say achieving those objectives will depend not only on attracting investment but also on protecting oil assets, tackling crude oil theft, and maintaining stability in the Niger Delta. The 2025 licensing round underscores Nigeria’s efforts to strengthen upstream investment and expand exploration activities. According to the NUPRC, 143 companies submitted 200 bids for 37 of the 50 blocks offered during the commercial bid phase. The regulator expects the exercise to support reserve growth and increase production over the next three years. Stakeholders, however, argue that increased investment must be matched by improved protection of oil infrastructure, as pipeline vandalism and crude oil theft remain key challenges to production growth. They also stress the importance of assigning surveillance responsibilities to organisations with experience in protecting oil assets. The Federal Government engaged Tantita Security Services Nigeria Ltd to support the protection of oil assets and enhance security across parts of the Niger Delta. President Bola Tinubu appointed Tantita Security Services Nigeria Limited, led by High Chief Government Ekpemupolo, alias Tompolo, to help safeguard Nigeria’s oil assets in the region. The company works alongside other security agencies in carrying out surveillance activities aimed at protecting oil facilities and supporting stability in the Niger Delta. Stakeholders say improved surveillance has contributed to efforts to reduce pipeline vandalism and crude oil theft while supporting more stable production levels. They also argue that protecting oil infrastructure remains critical to sustaining investment, improving production and supporting government revenue. The Chief Executive Officer of the Centre for the Promotion of Private Enterprise, Muda Yusuf, said Nigeria’s energy transition agenda makes it important to secure existing oil assets while expanding production. He described the 2025 licensing round as an effort to strengthen the country’s energy sector but said improved security must accompany investment initiatives. “We are in the age of energy transition. NUPRC’s operation in this exercise is good for the economy. It will boost foreign reserves, firm up the naira and macroeconomic environment while deepening foreign capital inflows to the domestic economy,” he said. Yusuf added that stronger protection of oil and gas infrastructure could encourage additional investment in the sector. “The new momentum generated will help build capacity to increase the level of production in the oil and gas industry,” he added. Abuja-based energy expert Steven Martins said the outcome of the licensing round represented more than a routine administrative exercise, noting that sustaining oil asset protection would be important as new investments move into the operational phase. “It represents a strategic recalibration of Nigeria’s energy ambitions at a time when global markets are shifting rapidly, and the country faces pressure to strengthen production, attract capital, and reposition itself in the era of energy transition. “As NUPRC is looking forward to harvesting an additional 500 million barrels to the national crude oil reserve from the 2025 bid round, these extra barrels are expected to be driven by the commencement of operational activities of the 31 companies that emerged as winners of 37 oil and gas blocks in the bid round licensing. Protection of oil assets should also form part of the priority areas for stakeholders, and that is where TSSNL should always be supported to achieve its goals,” he said. Martins explained that the awarded assets span several producing and frontier basins, including 16 Niger Delta Onshore blocks, 18 Niger Delta Shallow Water blocks, one Niger Delta Deep Offshore block, three Benin Basin Onshore blocks, four Anambra Basin Onshore blocks, four Chad Basin Onshore blocks and four Benue Trough blocks. The Chief Executive Officer, NUPRC, Mrs Oritsemeyiwa Eyesan, said at the Commercial Bid Conference for the Nigeria Licensing Round, themed “Expanding Opportunities: Right Play, Right Place, Right Time”, that the assets on offer have significant reserve potential. “The assets available in this licensing round have potential to add about 500 million barrels to Nigeria’s reserves, increasing our existing reserves of crude oil and condensate—which currently stand at 37.01 billion barrels—and 215.19 trillion cubic feet of gas,” she said. Eyesan said the absence of bids for the 13 frontier blocks was not unexpected. According to her, the assets presented during the 2025 commercial bid had previously been recovered from operators, while the commission would continue assessing other assets for possible inclusion in future licensing exercises. “The reason why we are in the market is because of the blocks that we have recovered from existing operators,” she said. Industry analysts also noted that the licensing round attracted notable investor interest in frontier basins. Some winners The companies that emerged as winners of the 2025 Licensing Round include SSonic Petroleum Limited (PPL 2A29), CFP Pipeline and Flowlines (2A30), Dutchford E&P Limited (2A32), Attabanson Global Company Limited (2A33 and PPL 901), Rosem Energy Limited (2A38), and Pivot-GIS Limited (2A39). The others include Network E&P (2A40), Asharami (2A41), LexOil (2A42), BVOF (2A43), GupscoEnergy Limited (2A44 and 2A51), Saratoga (2A45), Volante (2A46), Concept-Reel Petroleum Services Limited (2A47 and 2A55), Clinton Oil Field (2A48 and 2A62), Nuway Oaklane Limited (2A49), Ramec (2A50), Italia (2A53), Blueridge E&P (2A54), Up Energies Limited (2A56), and AYM Shafa (2A57). There are also Blackrock Holdings Limited (2A58), Funtay Integrated Business Limited (2A59), Riparian Development and Production Limited (2A60), Nikstallis (2A61 and PPL 900), Stardeep Petroleum (PPL 2010), Dakoda & U Limited (PPL 308 and PPL 800), Southborne Oil and Gas Limited (PPL 902), Lanaka Petroleum (PPL 903), Highban Resources Limited (PPL 700) and Eyre Energy Limited (PPL 801). Views from stakeholders Stakeholders have continued to emphasise the importance of pipeline protection to Nigeria’s oil production ambitions, arguing that safeguarding critical infrastructure remains essential to sustaining output, attracting investment and supporting broader economic growth. Related News Over 1,100 host community projects ongoing in Niger Delta, says NUPRC Oil block bidding: Inside Nigeria’s seven-hour transparency test REAN urges action to accelerate Nigeria’s clean energy transition In a published report, the President-General of the Niger Delta Progressive Alliance, Nse Udoh, argued that pipeline surveillance has helped national institutions move from reactive crisis management to more strategic planning and long-term decision-making. “It is important to clarify the role of pipeline surveillance within the wider energy landscape. Energy security encompasses the full value chain, from exploration and production to refining, distribution, pricing policy, and subsidy frameworks. Pipeline surveillance does not manage these domains,” he said. Udoh, however, maintained that pipeline surveillance plays a critical supporting role by protecting infrastructure that transports petroleum resources. “Its mandate is precise: safeguarding critical infrastructure that transports petroleum resources. Yet this single function has proven foundational. Without secure transportation channels, production targets falter, refining plans collapse, exports decline, and fiscal projections become unreliable,” he added. He further argued that asset protection underpins the efficient operation of the petroleum value chain. “Asset protection, in this context, is not a supporting activity. It is a precondition for economic order. In effect, the pipeline is the hinge on which the entire petroleum value chain turns. When that hinge is weak, every other link in the chain carries strain. When it is secure, the entire system gains coherence,” he wrote. According to him, sustained monitoring and rapid-response measures have contributed to fewer pipeline breaches and incidents of illegal tapping, while improvements in production have helped strengthen Nigeria’s position in the international oil market. Udoh also argued that greater certainty in crude oil transportation supports planning across the petroleum value chain. “Economic stability follows predictability. When crude flows are secure, refineries can plan feedstock intake with assurance. Export commitments can be met without fear of sudden shortfalls. Gas-to-power projects can operate without recurrent shutdown risks. “Investors can assess Nigeria’s petroleum sector with clearer risk profiles. Surveillance therefore does more than stop theft. It reintroduces reliability into national energy planning. And reliability is the bedrock upon which sustainable economic growth is built. “With predictable flows, national budgeting becomes more credible, infrastructure planning becomes more precise, and long-term contracts become easier to negotiate. Predictability is the silent currency of modern economies, and pipeline surveillance has begun restoring it,” he stated. He added that improved production accountability has supported export earnings, strengthened foreign exchange inflows and enhanced fiscal capacity, while helping national oil company operations become more efficient through lower losses and improved operational continuity. According to Udoh, more stable revenues also improve government planning by creating greater fiscal certainty and supporting investment in infrastructure, social services and economic diversification. Traditional rulers and community leaders in the Niger Delta have also called for continued support for Tantita Security Services Limited following a vote of confidence passed on the company by the National Assembly. The leaders said the company’s involvement in protecting oil and gas pipelines had coincided with improvements in security across some oil-producing communities, while also creating employment opportunities for young people. Commenting on the development, the President-General of the Isoko Development Union, Christopher Akpotu, commended the joint committee of the Senate and House of Representatives for dismissing petitions filed against the company. He described the decision as beneficial to the Niger Delta and urged stakeholders to focus on expanding opportunities in the oil and gas sector rather than internal disputes. “That is the right way to go,” he said. “There are many opportunities in the oil and gas sector. We should focus on how to derive more benefits rather than fighting over what has already been allocated.” Akpotu cautioned that prolonged disagreements among host communities could create opportunities for outsiders to benefit at their expense. “At the end of the day, if we continue fighting, we give room to those who have no stake in our communities to take what rightfully belongs to us,” he added. While acknowledging concerns about the distribution of benefits, he maintained that the surveillance contract had positively affected many communities. “The truth is that many communities and youths have benefited from the surveillance contract. It may not be evenly distributed, but the impact is evident,” he said. He also urged Tantita to broaden engagement with traditional rulers, youths and community leaders to promote fairness and strengthen long-term cooperation. Similarly, the President-General of the Ughelli Descendants Union, Sam Akpemegi, said security had improved since the company began operations, noting that both physical surveillance and intelligence-driven strategies had been deployed. “They have done very well and improved security since they began operations,” he said, adding that the company’s activities now extend across many communities. Akpemegi added that the company’s presence had benefited both traditional institutions and local residents, while calling for continued support for its operations. In the same vein, the Odiologbo of Ofagbe Kingdom, Ogaga Ikpoku, backed the National Assembly’s decision, describing it as timely and beneficial to peace and stability in the region. Stakeholders also argued that improved protection of oil assets has produced environmental and social benefits in parts of the Niger Delta. They noted that illegal refining and pipeline sabotage had previously polluted waterways, degraded farmlands and exposed communities to environmental and security risks, adding that sustained surveillance efforts against illegal refining sites and repeated pipeline breaches have supported ongoing environmental recovery. According to her, the assets presented during the 2025 commercial bid had previously been recovered from operators, while the commission would continue assessing other assets for possible inclusion in future licensing exercises. “The reason why we are in the market is because of the blocks that we have recovered from existing operators,” she said. Industry analysts also noted that the licensing round attracted notable investor interest in frontier basins. Some winners The companies that emerged as winners of the 2025 Licensing Round include SSonic Petroleum Limited (PPL 2A29), CFP Pipeline and Flowlines (2A30), Dutchford E&P Limited (2A32), Attabanson Global Company Limited (2A33 and PPL 901), Rosem Energy Limited (2A38), and Pivot-GIS Limited (2A39). The others include Network E&P (2A40), Asharami (2A41), LexOil (2A42), BVOF (2A43), GupscoEnergy Limited (2A44 and 2A51), Saratoga (2A45), Volante (2A46), Concept-Reel Petroleum Services Limited (2A47 and 2A55), Clinton Oil Field (2A48 and 2A62), Nuway Oaklane Limited (2A49), Ramec (2A50), Italia (2A53), Blueridge E&P (2A54), Up Energies Limited (2A56), and AYM Shafa (2A57). There are also Blackrock Holdings Limited (2A58), Funtay Integrated Business Limited (2A59), Riparian Development and Production Limited (2A60), Nikstallis (2A61 and PPL 900), Stardeep Petroleum (PPL 2010), Dakoda & U Limited (PPL 308 and PPL 800), Southborne Oil and Gas Limited (PPL 902), Lanaka Petroleum (PPL 903), Highban Resources Limited (PPL 700) and Eyre Energy Limited (PPL 801). Views from stakeholders Stakeholders have continued to emphasise the importance of pipeline protection to Nigeria’s oil production ambitions, arguing that safeguarding critical infrastructure remains essential to sustaining output, attracting investment and supporting broader economic growth. Related News Over 1,100 host community projects ongoing in Niger Delta, says NUPRC Oil block bidding: Inside Nigeria’s seven-hour transparency test REAN urges action to accelerate Nigeria’s clean energy transition In a published report, the President-General of the Niger Delta Progressive Alliance, Nse Udoh, argued that pipeline surveillance has helped national institutions move from reactive crisis management to more strategic planning and long-term decision-making. “It is important to clarify the role of pipeline surveillance within the wider energy landscape. Energy security encompasses the full value chain, from exploration and production to refining, distribution, pricing policy, and subsidy frameworks. Pipeline surveillance does not manage these domains,” he said. Udoh, however, maintained that pipeline surveillance plays a critical supporting role by protecting infrastructure that transports petroleum resources. “Its mandate is precise: safeguarding critical infrastructure that transports petroleum resources. Yet this single function has proven foundational. Without secure transportation channels, production targets falter, refining plans collapse, exports decline, and fiscal projections become unreliable,” he added. He further argued that asset protection underpins the efficient operation of the petroleum value chain. “Asset protection, in this context, is not a supporting activity. It is a precondition for economic order. In effect, the pipeline is the hinge on which the entire petroleum value chain turns. When that hinge is weak, every other link in the chain carries strain. When it is secure, the entire system gains coherence,” he wrote. According to him, sustained monitoring and rapid-response measures have contributed to fewer pipeline breaches and incidents of illegal tapping, while improvements in production have helped strengthen Nigeria’s position in the international oil market. Udoh also argued that greater certainty in crude oil transportation supports planning across the petroleum value chain. “Economic stability follows predictability. When crude flows are secure, refineries can plan feedstock intake with assurance. Export commitments can be met without fear of sudden shortfalls. Gas-to-power projects can operate without recurrent shutdown risks. “Investors can assess Nigeria’s petroleum sector with clearer risk profiles. Surveillance therefore does more than stop theft. It reintroduces reliability into national energy planning. And reliability is the bedrock upon which sustainable economic growth is built. “With predictable flows, national budgeting becomes more credible, infrastructure planning becomes more precise, and long-term contracts become easier to negotiate. Predictability is the silent currency of modern economies, and pipeline surveillance has begun restoring it,” he stated. He added that improved production accountability has supported export earnings, strengthened foreign exchange inflows and enhanced fiscal capacity, while helping national oil company operations become more efficient through lower losses and improved operational continuity. According to Udoh, more stable revenues also improve government planning by creating greater fiscal certainty and supporting investment in infrastructure, social services and economic diversification. Traditional rulers and community leaders in the Niger Delta have also called for continued support for Tantita Security Services Limited following a vote of confidence passed on the company by the National Assembly. The leaders said the company’s involvement in protecting oil and gas pipelines had coincided with improvements in security across some oil-producing communities, while also creating employment opportunities for young people. Commenting on the development, the President-General of the Isoko Development Union, Christopher Akpotu, commended the joint committee of the Senate and House of Representatives for dismissing petitions filed against the company. He described the decision as beneficial to the Niger Delta and urged stakeholders to focus on expanding opportunities in the oil and gas sector rather than internal disputes. “That is the right way to go,” he said. “There are many opportunities in the oil and gas sector. We should focus on how to derive more benefits rather than fighting over what has already been allocated.” Akpotu cautioned that prolonged disagreements among host communities could create opportunities for outsiders to benefit at their expense. “At the end of the day, if we continue fighting, we give room to those who have no stake in our communities to take what rightfully belongs to us,” he added. While acknowledging concerns about the distribution of benefits, he maintained that the surveillance contract had positively affected many communities. “The truth is that many communities and youths have benefited from the surveillance contract. It may not be evenly distributed, but the impact is evident,” he said. He also urged Tantita to broaden engagement with traditional rulers, youths and community leaders to promote fairness and strengthen long-term cooperation. Similarly, the President-General of the Ughelli Descendants Union, Sam Akpemegi, said security had improved since the company began operations, noting that both physical surveillance and intelligence-driven strategies had been deployed. “They have done very well and improved security since they began operations,” he said, adding that the company’s activities now extend across many communities. Akpemegi added that the company’s presence had benefited both traditional institutions and local residents, while calling for continued support for its operations. In the same vein, the Odiologbo of Ofagbe Kingdom, Ogaga Ikpoku, backed the National Assembly’s decision, describing it as timely and beneficial to peace and stability in the region. Stakeholders also argued that improved protection of oil assets has produced environmental and social benefits in parts of the Niger Delta. They noted that illegal refining and pipeline sabotage had previously polluted waterways, degraded farmlands and exposed communities to environmental and security risks, adding that sustained surveillance efforts against illegal refining sites and repeated pipeline breaches have supported ongoing environmental recovery. The 2025 licensing round underscores Nigeria’s efforts to strengthen upstream investment and expand exploration activities. According to the NUPRC, 143 companies submitted 200 bids for 37 of the 50 blocks offered during the commercial bid phase. The regulator expects the exercise to support reserve growth and increase production over the next three years. Stakeholders, however, argue that increased investment must be matched by improved protection of oil infrastructure, as pipeline vandalism and crude oil theft remain key challenges to production growth. They also stress the importance of assigning surveillance responsibilities to organisations with experience in protecting oil assets. The Federal Government engaged Tantita Security Services Nigeria Ltd to support the protection of oil assets and enhance security across parts of the Niger Delta. President Bola Tinubu appointed Tantita Security Services Nigeria Limited, led by High Chief Government Ekpemupolo, alias Tompolo, to help safeguard Nigeria’s oil assets in the region. The company works alongside other security agencies in carrying out surveillance activities aimed at protecting oil facilities and supporting stability in the Niger Delta. Stakeholders say improved surveillance has contributed to efforts to reduce pipeline vandalism and crude oil theft while supporting more stable production levels. They also argue that protecting oil infrastructure remains critical to sustaining investment, improving production and supporting government revenue. The Chief Executive Officer of the Centre for the Promotion of Private Enterprise, Muda Yusuf, said Nigeria’s energy transition agenda makes it important to secure existing oil assets while expanding production. He described the 2025 licensing round as an effort to strengthen the country’s energy sector but said improved security must accompany investment initiatives. “We are in the age of energy transition. NUPRC’s operation in this exercise is good for the economy. It will boost foreign reserves, firm up the naira and macroeconomic environment while deepening foreign capital inflows to the domestic economy,” he said. Yusuf added that stronger protection of oil and gas infrastructure could encourage additional investment in the sector. “The new momentum generated will help build capacity to increase the level of production in the oil and gas industry,” he added. Abuja-based energy expert Steven Martins said the outcome of the licensing round represented more than a routine administrative exercise, noting that sustaining oil asset protection would be important as new investments move into the operational phase. “It represents a strategic recalibration of Nigeria’s energy ambitions at a time when global markets are shifting rapidly, and the country faces pressure to strengthen production, attract capital, and reposition itself in the era of energy transition. “As NUPRC is looking forward to harvesting an additional 500 million barrels to the national crude oil reserve from the 2025 bid round, these extra barrels are expected to be driven by the commencement of operational activities of the 31 companies that emerged as winners of 37 oil and gas blocks in the bid round licensing. Protection of oil assets should also form part of the priority areas for stakeholders, and that is where TSSNL should always be supported to achieve its goals,” he said. Martins explained that the awarded assets span several producing and frontier basins, including 16 Niger Delta Onshore blocks, 18 Niger Delta Shallow Water blocks, one Niger Delta Deep Offshore block, three Benin Basin Onshore blocks, four Anambra Basin Onshore blocks, four Chad Basin Onshore blocks and four Benue Trough blocks. The Chief Executive Officer, NUPRC, Mrs Oritsemeyiwa Eyesan, said at the Commercial Bid Conference for the Nigeria Licensing Round, themed “Expanding Opportunities: Right Play, Right Place, Right Time”, that the assets on offer have significant reserve potential. “The assets available in this licensing round have potential to add about 500 million barrels to Nigeria’s reserves, increasing our existing reserves of crude oil and condensate—which currently stand at 37.01 billion barrels—and 215.19 trillion cubic feet of gas,” she said. Eyesan said the absence of bids for the 13 frontier blocks was not unexpected. According to her, the assets presented during the 2025 commercial bid had previously been recovered from operators, while the commission would continue assessing other assets for possible inclusion in future licensing exercises. “The reason why we are in the market is because of the blocks that we have recovered from existing operators,” she said. Industry analysts also noted that the licensing round attracted notable investor interest in frontier basins. The others include Network E&P (2A40), Asharami (2A41), LexOil (2A42), BVOF (2A43), GupscoEnergy Limited (2A44 and 2A51), Saratoga (2A45), Volante (2A46), Concept-Reel Petroleum Services Limited (2A47 and 2A55), Clinton Oil Field (2A48 and 2A62), Nuway Oaklane Limited (2A49), Ramec (2A50), Italia (2A53), Blueridge E&P (2A54), Up Energies Limited (2A56), and AYM Shafa (2A57). There are also Blackrock Holdings Limited (2A58), Funtay Integrated Business Limited (2A59), Riparian Development and Production Limited (2A60), Nikstallis (2A61 and PPL 900), Stardeep Petroleum (PPL 2010), Dakoda & U Limited (PPL 308 and PPL 800), Southborne Oil and Gas Limited (PPL 902), Lanaka Petroleum (PPL 903), Highban Resources Limited (PPL 700) and Eyre Energy Limited (PPL 801).
